     1→"""QLoRA training orchestrator.
     2→
     3→All GPU-dependent imports (datasets, transformers, trl, torch) are deferred
     4→inside function bodies to ensure CPU-only importability (INFRA-05).
     5→
     6→Module-level imports: stdlib only.
     7→"""
     8→
     9→from __future__ import annotations
    10→
    11→import hashlib
    12→import os
    13→from datetime import datetime, timezone
    14→from pathlib import Path
    15→
    16→
    17→def train_qlora(
    18→    session_id: str,
    19→    adapter_id: str,
    20→    output_dir: str,
    21→    *,
    22→    base_model_id: str | None = None,
    23→    task_type: str = "code-gen",
    24→    rank: int = 64,
    25→    alpha: int = 128,
    26→    epochs: int = 3,
    27→    learning_rate: float = 2e-4,
    28→) -> str:
    29→    """Train a QLoRA adapter from a recorded coding trajectory.
    30→
    31→    Orchestrates the full training pipeline: load trajectory, format as SFT
    32→    messages, build dataset, load model with NF4 quantization, train with SFT,
    33→    and save the adapter to output_dir.
    34→
    35→    All GPU imports are deferred to this function body; the module is safe to
    36→    import in CPU-only environments.
    37→
    38→    Args:
    39→        session_id: Trajectory session ID to train from.
    40→        adapter_id: Unique identifier for the resulting adapter.
    41→        output_dir: Directory to save the trained adapter weights.
    42→        base_model_id: HuggingFace model ID. Defaults to RUNE_BASE_MODEL env var
    43→            or "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct".
    44→        task_type: Task category (e.g. 'code-gen', 'bug-fix').
    45→        rank: LoRA rank.
    46→        alpha: LoRA alpha scaling factor.
    47→        epochs: Number of training epochs.
    48→        learning_rate: Optimizer learning rate.
    49→
    50→    Returns:
    51→        output_dir path where the adapter was saved.
    52→
    53→    Raises:
    54→        FileNotFoundError: If the trajectory file does not exist.
    55→        ValueError: If the trajectory is not successful or has no SFT messages.
    56→    """
    57→    # Deferred GPU imports — all in function body for CPU-only importability
    58→    import torch
    59→    from datasets import Dataset
    60→    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer, BitsAndBytesConfig
    61→    from trl import SFTConfig, SFTTrainer
    62→
    63→    from model_training.peft_utils import build_qlora_config
    64→    from model_training.trajectory import format_for_sft, load_trajectory
    65→
    66→    # Resolve model ID — read env var inside function body for monkeypatch testability
    67→    model_id = base_model_id or os.environ.get(
    68→        "RUNE_BASE_MODEL", "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct"
    69→    )
    70→
    71→    # Load and format trajectory
    72→    trajectory = load_trajectory(session_id)  # raises FileNotFoundError if missing
    73→    messages = format_for_sft(trajectory)
    74→    if not messages:
    75→        raise ValueError(
    76→            f"Trajectory {session_id} is not successful or has no SFT messages"
    77→        )
    78→
    79→    # Build dataset
    80→    dataset = Dataset.from_list([{"messages": messages}])
    81→
    82→    # NF4 quantization config (bfloat16 compute dtype prevents silent NaN loss)
    83→    bnb_config = BitsAndBytesConfig(
    84→        load_in_4bit=True,
    85→        bnb_4bit_quant_type="nf4",
    86→        bnb_4bit_compute_dtype=torch.bfloat16,
    87→        bnb_4bit_use_double_quant=True,
    88→    )
    89→
    90→    # Load model and tokenizer
    91→    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
    92→        model_id,
    93→        quantization_config=bnb_config,
    94→        device_map="auto",
    95→    )
    96→    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(model_id)
    97→
    98→    # Build LoRA config (bfloat16 is set in BitsAndBytesConfig, not LoraConfig)
    99→    lora_config = build_qlora_config(
   100→        rank=rank,
   101→        alpha=alpha,
   102→        target_modules=["q_proj", "v_proj"],
   103→        dropout=0.1,
   104→    )
   105→
   106→    # Training arguments
   107→    training_args = SFTConfig(
   108→        output_dir=output_dir,
   109→        num_train_epochs=epochs,
   110→        learning_rate=learning_rate,
   111→        warmup_ratio=0.03,
   112→        lr_scheduler_type="cosine",
   113→        bf16=True,
   114→        per_device_train_batch_size=1,
   115→        gradient_accumulation_steps=4,
   116→        save_strategy="no",
   117→        logging_steps=1,
   118→        report_to="none",
   119→        eval_strategy="no",
   120→    )
   121→
   122→    # Create and run trainer
   123→    trainer = SFTTrainer(
   124→        model=model,
   125→        args=training_args,
   126→        train_dataset=dataset,
   127→        peft_config=lora_config,
   128→        processing_class=tokenizer,
   129→    )
   130→    trainer.train()
   131→
   132→    # Save adapter weights only (PEFT-aware: safetensors + adapter_config.json)
   133→    trainer.save_model(output_dir)
   134→
   135→    return output_dir
   136→
   137→
   138→def train_and_register(
   139→    session_id: str,
   140→    adapter_id: str,
   141→    *,
   142→    base_model_id: str | None = None,
   143→    task_type: str = "code-gen",
   144→    rank: int = 64,
   145→    alpha: int = 128,
   146→    epochs: int = 3,
   147→    learning_rate: float = 2e-4,
   148→    database_url: str | None = None,
   149→) -> str:
   150→    """Train a QLoRA adapter and register it in the AdapterRegistry.
   151→
   152→    Combines train_qlora() with AdapterRegistry.store() to produce a fully
   153→    registered adapter ready for vLLM serving.
   154→
   155→    Args:
   156→        session_id: Trajectory session ID to train from.
   157→        adapter_id: Unique identifier for the resulting adapter.
   158→        base_model_id: HuggingFace model ID. Defaults to RUNE_BASE_MODEL env var.
   159→        task_type: Task category (e.g. 'code-gen', 'bug-fix').
   160→        rank: LoRA rank.
   161→        alpha: LoRA alpha scaling factor.
   162→        epochs: Number of training epochs.
   163→        learning_rate: Optimizer learning rate.
   164→        database_url: SQLAlchemy database URL. Defaults to RUNE_DATABASE_URL env var
   165→            or "sqlite:///{home}/.rune/rune.db".
   166→
   167→    Returns:
   168→        adapter_id of the registered adapter.
   169→
   170→    Raises:
   171→        FileNotFoundError: If the trajectory file does not exist.
   172→        ValueError: If the trajectory is not successful or has no SFT messages.
   173→    """
   174→    from adapter_registry.models import AdapterRecord
   175→    from adapter_registry.registry import AdapterRegistry
   176→    from sqlalchemy import create_engine
   177→
   178→    # Resolve adapter output dir — read env var inside function body
   179→    adapter_base = os.environ.get("RUNE_ADAPTER_DIR")
   180→    if adapter_base:
   181→        adapter_dir = Path(adapter_base) / adapter_id
   182→    else:
   183→        adapter_dir = Path.home() / ".rune" / "adapters" / adapter_id
   184→    adapter_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   185→
   186→    # Resolve model ID inside function body for testability
   187→    model_id = base_model_id or os.environ.get(
   188→        "RUNE_BASE_MODEL", "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct"
   189→    )
   190→
   191→    output_dir = str(adapter_dir)
   192→    train_qlora(
   193→        session_id=session_id,
   194→        adapter_id=adapter_id,
   195→        output_dir=output_dir,
   196→        base_model_id=model_id,
   197→        task_type=task_type,
   198→        rank=rank,
   199→        alpha=alpha,
   200→        epochs=epochs,
   201→        learning_rate=learning_rate,
   202→    )
   203→
   204→    # Compute file hash and size from the saved safetensors file
   205→    adapter_file = adapter_dir / "adapter_model.safetensors"
   206→    file_hash = hashlib.sha256(adapter_file.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
   207→    file_size_bytes = adapter_file.stat().st_size
   208→
   209→    # Build the adapter record
   210→    record = AdapterRecord(
   211→        id=adapter_id,
   212→        version=1,
   213→        task_type=task_type,
   214→        base_model_id=model_id,
   215→        rank=rank,
   216→        created_at=datetime.now(tz=timezone.utc).isoformat(),
   217→        file_path=output_dir,
   218→        file_hash=file_hash,
   219→        file_size_bytes=file_size_bytes,
   220→        source="qlora",
   221→        session_id=session_id,
   222→    )
   223→
   224→    # Resolve DB URL inside function body for testability
   225→    db_url = database_url or os.environ.get(
   226→        "RUNE_DATABASE_URL",
   227→        f"sqlite:///{Path.home() / '.rune' / 'rune.db'}",
   228→    )
   229→    engine = create_engine(db_url)
   230→    registry = AdapterRegistry(engine=engine)
   231→    registry.store(record)
   232→
   233→    return adapter_id
   234→
```

     3→All GPU library imports (peft, transformers, torch) are deferred inside
     4→function bodies to ensure CPU-only importability (INFRA-05).

     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→from typing import Any
    10→
    11→
    12→def build_qlora_config(
    13→    rank: int,
    14→    alpha: int,
    15→    target_modules: list[str],
    16→    dropout: float = 0.1,
    17→) -> Any:
    18→    """Build a QLoRA configuration for PEFT fine-tuning.
    19→
    20→    Args:
    21→        rank: LoRA rank (dimensionality of low-rank matrices).
    22→        alpha: LoRA alpha scaling factor.
    23→        target_modules: List of module names to apply LoRA to.
    24→        dropout: Dropout probability for LoRA layers.
    25→
    26→    Returns:
    27→        A peft LoraConfig instance configured for QLoRA.
    28→
    29→    Example:
    30→        >>> config = build_qlora_config(rank=64, alpha=128, target_modules=["q_proj"])

    34→    return LoraConfig(
    35→        r=rank,
    36→        lora_alpha=alpha,
    37→        target_modules=target_modules,
    38→        lora_dropout=dropout,
    39→        bias="none",
    40→        task_type="CAUSAL_LM",
    41→    )
    42→
    43→
    44→def apply_lora_adapter(model: Any, config: Any) -> Any:
    45→    """Apply a LoRA adapter to a base model.
    46→

    49→        config: The LoRA configuration (from build_qlora_config).
    50→
    51→    Returns:
    52→        The model wrapped with a LoRA adapter via peft.get_peft_model.
    53→
    54→    Example:
    55→        >>> adapted_model = apply_lora_adapter(base_model, lora_config)
    56→    """
    57→    from peft import get_peft_model  # deferred — GPU/peft not available in CPU CI
    58→
    59→    return get_peft_model(model, config)

     1→# Rune
     2→
     3→**Treating LoRA weight space as episodic memory for unbounded context in local coding agents**
     4→
     5→---
     6→
     7→## Vision
     8→
     9→### The Context Window Problem
    10→
    11→Current coding agents face a fundamental architectural constraint: as codebases grow, token-based context windows become bloated, expensive, and ultimately insufficient. Even with 400K-token windows, maintaining state across long coding sessions requires quadratic compute overhead and suffers from "needle in a haystack" retrieval failures. For local Small Language Models (SLMs) under 10B parameters, this limitation is existential.
    12→
    13→Rune addresses this by **moving state representation from the token-level context window into the model's weight-space** via dynamic, task-specific LoRA adapters. Rather than stuffing ever-more tokens into context, we encode procedural knowledge—architecture specifications, execution feedback, coding conventions—directly into the model's parameters as modular, composable memory units.
    14→
    15→### The Solution: Parametric Episodic Memory
    16→
    17→Rune implements a **recursive code generation loop** where each iteration:
    18→
    19→1. **Generates code** using the current adapter state
    20→2. **Executes** in a sandboxed environment
    21→3. **Distills feedback** (errors, test results, architectural constraints) into LoRA weight updates via a hypernetwork
    22→4. **Composes memory** by merging task-specific adapters into a persistent library
    23→
    24→**Initial Bootstrapping**: Before the first code generation iteration, the hypernetwork processes the project specification—goals, implementation rules, architecture conventions, and organizational structure—to create an initial project-specific adapter. This ensures the base model is immediately contextualized to the project's requirements, rather than starting from a generic coding baseline.
    25→
    26→This transforms coding from a stateless LLM call into a **sequential decision-making process with parameter-space memory**. The agent doesn't just retrieve documentation—it internalizes procedural logic as trainable weight deltas, creating "skill-based memory" that persists across sessions.
    27→
    28→**Core Innovation**: We treat the LoRA weight space as episodic memory. Each coding trajectory becomes a reusable adapter that captures not just what was done, but how to do it. By composing these adapters, the agent builds an unbounded, hierarchical memory structure that scales independently of context window size.
    29→
    30→---
    31→
    32→## Architecture Overview
    33→
    34→### The Recursive Loop
    35→
    36→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    37→│  Base SLM (<10B params) + Active LoRA Adapter(s)       │
    38→└──────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────┘
    39→                   │
    40→                   ▼
    41→         ┌─────────────────┐
    42→         │  Code Generation │
    43→         └────────┬─────────┘
    44→                  │
    45→                  ▼
    46→         ┌─────────────────┐
    47→         │ Sandbox Execution│
    48→         └────────┬─────────┘
    49→                  │
    50→                  ▼
    51→    ┌────────────────────────────┐
    52→    │  System-2 Reasoning Loop   │
    53→    │  (Error Analysis + Logic   │
    54→    │   Correction + Test Review)│
    55→    └─────────┬──────────────────┘
    56→              │
    57→              ▼
    58→    ┌──────────────────────────┐
    59→    │  Doc-to-LoRA Hypernetwork│
    60→    │  (Feedback → ΔW encoding)│
    61→    └──────────┬───────────────┘
    62→               │
    63→               ▼
    64→    ┌──────────────────────────┐
    65→    │   Evolution Operator (E) │
    66→    │  (Consolidate/Update/    │
    67→    │   Forget/Merge Adapters) │
    68→    └──────────┬───────────────┘
    69→               │
    70→               └──────► Update Active Adapters
    71→                        │
    72→                        └──► Persist to Adapter Library
    73→
    74→### Key Components
    75→
    76→**1. Doc-to-LoRA Hypernetwork**
    77→
    78→A lightweight meta-learning network that maps execution traces to LoRA parameters, inspired by Sakana AI's Doc-to-LoRA architecture[44]:
    79→
    80→- **Input**: Project documentation, architecture specs, error traces, test results
    81→- **Output**: Rank-decomposed LoRA matrices (ΔW = BA, where B ∈ ℝ^(d×r), A ∈ ℝ^(r×d))
    82→- **Architecture**: Perceiver-based encoder to handle variable-length inputs, chunk mechanism for contexts exceeding base model window
    83→- **Training**: Meta-trained on diverse coding trajectories to learn the mapping from feedback → parameter updates
    84→
    85→**Initial Pass Usage**: Unlike the original Doc-to-LoRA which focuses on knowledge updates, Rune extends this to project initialization. On the first invocation, the hypernetwork processes project specifications (goals, coding standards, architecture patterns, file organization) to generate an initial adapter that contextualizes the base model to the specific project domain before any code is generated.
    86→
    87→**2. Evolution Operator**
    88→
    89→Manages adapter lifecycle with three operations:
    90→
    91→- **Consolidate**: Merge related adapters (e.g., "React hooks" + "useEffect error handling" → "React state management")
    92→- **Update**: Refine existing adapters with new feedback
    93→- **Forget**: Prune low-utility adapters to maintain global coherence
    94→- **Conflict Resolution**: Detects architectural misalignments between adapters using structural graph analysis
    95→
    96→**3. Dynamic Adapter Serving**
    97→
    98→Built on S-LoRA principles for scalable multi-adapter hosting:
    99→
   100→- **Unified Paging**: Shared memory pool for KV cache + adapter weights, reduces fragmentation
```

I now have a deep understanding of Rune from reading all the docs. Let me synthesize what I know and what's missing for the E2E flow.

## What I understand

**Rune's core thesis**: LoRA adapters as episodic memory. Each coding session produces a trajectory → that trajectory gets distilled into a LoRA adapter → future sessions load relevant adapters → the model accumulates project knowledge in weight-space, not context-space.

**The full intended flow**:
1. **Project bootstrapping** — hypernetwork processes project spec → initial adapter contextualizes the model
2. **Task decomposition** — project broken into coding tasks with test suites
3. **Swarm execution** — agents run tasks through LangGraph loop (generate → execute → reflect → retry)
4. **Trajectory distillation** — successful trajectories → LoRA adapters (Phase 3: QLoRA fine-tuning, Phase 4: hypernetwork forward pass)
5. **Adapter feedback** — subsequent tasks load best adapters from registry → model improves
6. **Evolution** — merge/prune adapters based on fitness

**What exists (implemented)**:
- LangGraph agent loop (generate/execute/reflect/save_trajectory) ✅
- Inference providers (vLLM/Ollama) ✅
- Sandbox execution (subprocess/nsjail) ✅
- Trajectory recording + SFT formatting ✅
- QLoRA trainer (`train_qlora`, `train_and_register`) ✅
- Hypernetwork architecture (DocToLoraHypernetwork) ✅
- Adapter registry with lineage/fitness ✅
- Swarm orchestrator shell (swarm.py) ✅
- Evolution worker + TIES/DARE merging ✅

**What's missing for the E2E test**:
1. **Project decomposition** — no code exists to take a project description and break it into tasks with test suites
2. **Agent supervisor doesn't use LangGraph** — currently runs code directly, not through LLM
3. **No project bootstrapping step** — hypernetwork exists but nothing wires project spec → initial adapter
4. **Hardware constraint** — M4 Pro has no CUDA, so QLoRA training can't actually run. But Ollama inference works fine.

Let me think about what's feasible for a real test and design the plan.

I am not sure you get it fully. Our goal is to iterate on a task not improve for next time. The way we converge on a solution is by passing trajectory (diff), what we tried, what were the results of our attempt via hypernetworks into the model so we don't use up context window.

> AGENT

I understand now. The hypernetwork isn't for cross-task improvement — it's the **context mechanism within each task's iteration loop**. Instead of stuffing prior attempts into the prompt (filling context), each iteration's trajectory gets encoded as a LoRA adapter via the hypernetwork, and the next attempt loads that adapter. The model "remembers" what it tried through weight-space, not token-space. The prompt stays constant-size.

Current loop (context fills up):
```
attempt 1: generate(prompt) → fail → stuff error into prompt
attempt 2: generate(prompt + prior_code + error) → fail → stuff more
attempt 3: generate(prompt + ALL prior attempts) → context blows up
```

**Rune's loop (constant context)**:
```
attempt 1: generate(prompt, adapter=none) → fail → hypernetwork(trajectory) → adapter_v1
attempt 2: generate(prompt, adapter=adapter_v1) → fail → hypernetwork(trajectory) → adapter_v2  
attempt 3: generate(prompt, adapter=adapter_v2) → works! Context never grew.
```

Let me rewrite the plan with this correct understanding.

     3→## Git / CI Rules
     4→- **Never commit `.planning/` directories** — user explicitly requested this

     9→## Key Architecture
    10→- Python monorepo: libs/ (shared packages) + services/ (FastAPI microservices)
    11→- Build: uv workspace with hatchling backends
    12→- Testing: pytest with xdist (12 workers), pytest-cov
    13→- Linting: ruff (check + format) + mypy strict
    14→- GPU deps (torch, peft, safetensors, openai) are optional — CI uses mock injection patterns
    15→
    16→## CI Patterns
    17→- `openai` must be in root pyproject.toml deps for CI (inference lib needs it)
    18→- mypy overrides for GPU-only packages: torch, peft, safetensors, bitsandbytes, openai, etc.
    19→- Inference `__init__.py` uses lazy `__getattr__` for OllamaProvider/VLLMProvider (avoid openai import)
    20→- Hypernetwork tests use `_ensure_safetensors_module()` for safe patching without safetensors installed
    21→- Test injection pattern: `sys.modules["package"] = ModuleType("package")` for faking GPU deps
    22→
    23→## Workflows
    24→- PR code review: `.github/workflows/code-review.yml` (Claude + Copilot)
    25→- CI: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (lint, type-check, test, docs-build)

This still isn't 100% correct. The first attempt uses the hypernetwork to pass the general method for planning, executing, coding and validating to the model. > AGENT

NOW I get the full picture. The hypernetwork carries **everything** — not just retry context, but the entire project lifecycle state:

```
Iteration 0: methodology doc → H() → methodology_adapter
  (model now knows HOW to plan/code/validate projects)

Iteration 1: prompt="Build a stats library" + adapter=methodology_adapter
  → model outputs an implementation plan
  → H(plan trajectory) → plan_adapter
  (model now knows the plan through weights, not tokens)

Iteration 2: same prompt + adapter=plan_adapter  
  → model codes task 1 from the plan
  → execute → results
  → H(code+results trajectory) → progress_adapter_v1

Iteration 3: same prompt + adapter=progress_adapter_v1
  → model codes task 2 (knows task 1 is done, what worked)
  → H(trajectory) → progress_adapter_v2

...and so on. Prompt NEVER changes. ALL context lives in weight-space.
```

The prompt is just the launch trigger. The hypernetwork is the **entire state machine** — methodology, plan, progress, errors, everything encoded as adapter weights.

Prompts CAN be adjusted between iterations if useful, but the adapter is the primary context mechanism — the prompt doesn't need to grow to carry prior state.\n```\n\nThe swarm: multiple agents work on different plan tasks simultaneously, each with their own adapter chain.
